# SkipWait decision register (single source of truth)

Every owner decision lives here. Any doc, code, copy, test or config that disagrees with an **active** entry is wrong and must be fixed (or the doc marked "Superseded by D-xxx"). Read this with `AGENTS.md` section 0 and `docs/KIT_V4_BUILD_PLAN.md`.

## How decisions are made (binding, owner Oct 14 2026)
**Every platform and agent (Claude, Codex, Gemini, Copilot, Instinct, Devin, humans):** before merging any change that alters product behaviour, auth, money, data or copy, check it against this register; if it embodies a new decision, add the entry (or ask the owner) in the same PR.
1. **Audit first.** Inspect the project area and the evidence before proposing anything.
2. **Propose with evidence.** Each proposal states: the problem; data (production metrics/analytics, with the query or source), research (cited URL, publisher, year), behavioural psychology (the seeker's and the referrer's mental model at that step and the principle used), proven patterns from comparable products; the metric it moves and the expected effect; cost, risk and alternatives; and how it will be measured after release.
3. **Impact check.** List every older decision (by ID) and every doc, code area, copy, test and config the proposal touches or contradicts. Never silently override an older decision — the owner chooses which wins.
4. **Owner approves manually.** Ask targeted questions whenever intent, scope or a trade-off is unclear. No new change, redesign, pricing or process change is coded before explicit approval. Exceptions: P0 incidents (fix, then report) and tasks already approved in the queue.
5. **Record, then sync the whole project.** Add the entry here first (docs first), then update every impacted doc/code/copy/test in the same change set or as queued tasks. An entry is `synced` only when nothing in the repo contradicts it.
6. **Measure.** After release, record before/after numbers against the metric named in the entry.

Ethics guardrail for every psychology-based proposal: no dark patterns, false urgency, fabricated proof or scarcity, guilt-tripping, or pressure on referrers; referring always stays voluntary; real numbers only.

## Active decisions
IDs are unique and never reused: immediately before committing a new entry, fetch `main` and every open branch (`git fetch origin`, then `git show <branch>:docs/DECISIONS.md` for each `origin/*` branch), take the highest existing ID + 1 across them, and re-check after a rebase. If two open branches hold one ID, the first to reach `main` keeps it and the later one renumbers every reference in its own merge (D-162: an open branch can already hold the next ID).

| D-035 | 2026-10-16 | **REJECTED by founder Ayu, Oct 23 (D-147) (attribution unverified - no recovered owner original; register state and refund behavior unchanged); historical proposal below, not implementation authority.** **A referrer decline shows a one-tap anonymous reason, and the reason decides the credit.** Reasons are one tap (optional note), split into *fixable* (profile incomplete, skill not shown, note unclear, level mismatch) and *not fixable* (role closed, can only refer people I know, wrong team). The seeker sees "A verified employee at <Company> said: <reason>", never the referrer's name. A not-fixable reason releases the credit; a fixable reason keeps it spent, as payment for the review. After fixing the flagged item the seeker may "Send again" for 1 credit, once per job, and only when the flagged item actually changed. The resend goes to colleagues, and to the same referrer only if they opted in. D-021 is unchanged: the declined ask still reopens to colleagues. Measure: decline-with-reason rate, fix rate, resend vs first-send acceptance, decline vs ignore rate before and after. | Owner's own proposal Oct 16 ("show the rejected with reason to correct in users profile and again they can send once corrected. again costs another credit"), approved with the guardrails above. Rivals refund undelivered referrals (RefOpen holds the charge "until a referral is actually made"; FindMyReferral escrow; seen Oct 16). Pay-per-attempt analogs draw the worst sentiment (Thumbtack 2.1/5 on Trustpilot). A charge reads as fair when its cost is visible (Kahneman, Knetsch & Thaler 1986). Loss aversion λ ≈ 1.96 (Brown et al., JEL 2024). | Supersedes D-012(2) and D-021 "the credit stays spent" for not-fixable declines; keeps them for fixable ones. Affects every decline surface, the seeker thread, notifications, and the referrer decline card (`CandidatePreviewCard.tsx:72`, which today wrongly says the credit is returned). | **NOT APPROVED (D-147, Oct 23): any employee decline keeps the credit spent, regardless of reason. | D-076 | 2026-10-18 | **Rulings inside the approved money rules, so no implementer has to guess (decided under D-074; each changeable by the owner).** (1) **"Released" credit** (D-035 not-fixable pass, D-038 lapses, D-066 step back): the pickup that consumed the credit is undone. The ask reopens with its one credit still attached and unspent. The credit comes back once if the reopened ask is withdrawn or expires unclaimed, and only the next pickup spends it again. No ledger row moves at release. The seeker is never charged twice, and an ask is never delivered free. Refund eligibility becomes "no claim since the latest release". (2) **Claim lapse (D-038a):** 48 h from the claim, with a reminder at 24 h (in-app now, email with D-060). The lapse is a recorded transition: it clears the referrer, releases their slot, excludes that referrer from the ask for good and tells the seeker plainly. It is not a pass: no reason, no "passed" notice. The 5-claim cap counts open claims only. (3) **Allocation lapse:** a delivered or queue-allocated ask that its referrer does not claim, accept or pass within 48 h of first visibility leaves that referrer the way a claim lapse does. It returns to the company's open queue for every available colleague, like a D-021 pass; no new credit is spent and that referrer is excluded. The 7-day expiry from first visibility still bounds the ask; the clock does not restart (as in D-021 and D-061). (4) **Referred deadline (D-038b):** 5 days from acceptance, reminders on day 2 and day 4, and one "need 3 more days" (the deadline becomes day 8). "Referred" is the referrer's mark or the seeker's "I got the referral email"; either one stops the clock and moves the ask to `intro_made`. A missed deadline, or D-066's "I can't refer this after all", reopens the ask to colleagues with the credit released as in (1), revokes the document grant and excludes that referrer. (5) **No retroactive storms:** for claims and acceptances that exist at deploy, the 48 h and 5-day clocks start at deploy time. (6) **Send again (D-035):** offered only after a fixable pass, once per seeker and job link, and only when, after the pass, the ask note was rewritten, the profile was updated or a new resume was uploaded (enforced on the server). It closes the reopened original without a refund (it was picked up and stays spent) and creates a new ask that spends 1 credit under the normal D-034 rule (D-066(1)). The new ask goes to colleagues, and to the passing referrer only if they ticked "send it back to me if they update it" when they passed (default off). (7) **Legacy passes:** a pass without one of the seven reason codes counts as not fixable, so the credit is released. (8) **What counts as a referral (D-066(3)):** an ask is referred when it reaches `intro_made` or later through the referrer's mark or the seeker's confirmation. Every public or wall "referral" number counts only those; "accepted" numbers say accepted. (9) **North-star clock (D-038):** "referred within 7 days" counts from first visibility (`coverageVisibleAt`, else creation). | Found while writing the D-038/D-035 packets (`docs/plans/`). Each ruling follows the decisions' stated intent (seekers never pay for a failed pickup, referrers are never pressured or paid, no free delivery per D-047) and the existing ledger design (keyed, replay-safe rows). | Refines D-035, D-038, D-052, D-061, D-066 and D-021's credit clause. No price, plan-content or new charge (D-056 allows building approved money rules). Workflow bot commits (the `[skip ci]` sync log) are exempt. | D-085 | 2026-10-20 | **How the agents work together, and who does what — in effect now.** Owner, verbatim: "if i tell multiple agents to work (by giving commands resume,continue or build) multiple model agents should collaborate and take next task not take the same task and conflict. make our functioning well organised"; "if soemthing is half built and not completely built and no one is working on it. the next agent should work on the half built one instead of taking new one. so that the priority tasks are always built first over next. half built priority tasks are always handled first there should never be an incident priority from top tasks are pending but the lower priority ones are getting executed"; "always check the local and repo . make sure they are always in sync (if something is working and not green yet . it is not currently handled by anyone the next agent should handle)"; "you(claude) always work like ceo in the architecting high stakes decision making,direction, handling entire business to earn money as well as give value to users, re analysing what we are building to make our platform goto global platform for referrals, what to do , what we are doing , what can be improved, like a ceo . im using cto like open frontier models to develop"; "implementing the CEO/engineering-team role split should be immediate"; "work like a organisation, all open models (which are cto,cdo, and all other) should report about all green priority status to ceo(claude). You ceo(claude decide the direction and intelligence to reach our goal of to be the first website for everyone to get who ever searches for job referrals) it should satisfy users and earn money for us"; "every agent should know,in sync on our priorities top to down , whether green or not . top priorities should always be green before touching the next priority"; "completing a pending task is important than taking new one"; and "always make sure local and git repo are in sync". So: (1) `build`, `resume`, `continue` and `next` all mean one thing: run `node scripts/next-task.mjs --claim "<model name>"` and do only what it prints; nobody resumes or chooses work from memory. A name with no ` · <session>` part gets a unique session tag from the picker (for example `Qwen 3 · k3f9`), which it prints; the agent uses exactly that full name for `--heartbeat`, `--report`, `--fixed`, `--verdict` and on resume (read from the holder column of `--status`, never invented), and two sessions never share a name. (2) Your own unfinished work comes back first (a build, a review, findings to fix, a landing, a packet, the red-main fix), so a restarted agent never leaves a half-built claim idle. Except: pending work ranked above your own task with nobody on it (a review, findings to fix, a red pull request whose owner is quiet, a landing, a half-built claim quiet for 2 hours, an unblock for the CEO) is handed to you first; you push your own work first (the picker enforces it), and your own claim stays yours for 2 hours. (3) Then finishing comes before starting: one top-down pass over Claude Code's order for pending work nobody is on — a review, findings to fix, a pull request with red CI, a green pull request nobody lands, a half-built claim, a blocked task — and only then a second top-down pass for new work. A priority that is red (red CI, or its team reported red or blocked) holds everything below it for everyone else: no new or pending work below it starts; an agent already holding work below it whose owner is active keeps going on its own work (its hands cannot help the red one); a red priority with nobody on it is handed out first; new work ranked above it may still start, and a blocked task goes to the CEO to unblock. Red `main` still comes before everything. (4) "Nobody is on it" means no commit and no heartbeat (`--heartbeat --as`) pushed for 2 hours (queue rule 2 was 24 hours); agents push after every commit, heartbeat before long steps, and commit and push before they stop. A holder that comes back after 90 minutes or more re-takes its claim on the same branch a takeover would push to, so the first push wins and two agents never both hold it. A claim, takeover, review, fix or landing is a marker pushed to origin, so two agents racing for one task cannot both win. (5) Local and origin stay in sync: the picker reads the local clone, refuses new work while your own claimed branch has unpushed commits or uncommitted changes, and reports any other local-only work and a stale local `main`. (6) **Roles:** Claude Code is the CEO — direction, high-stakes decisions, architecture, the business (revenue and user value), the continuous re-analysis of what we build toward the go-to global referral platform, packets, Tier 1 reviews and the queue order; the open frontier models are the CTO and engineering team and build from packets. The picker enforces it: Claude Code always claims as `--claim "Claude Code"` (any name starting with "Claude" is the CEO) and is offered red `main`, its own unfinished work, unblocks of blocked priorities, packets, Tier 1 reviews and Tier 1 landings after a passed review, never a build, takeover, make-green or findings fix, and the CEO review when nothing at its level waits; below a red priority it only writes packets and unblocks. (7) **The organisation reports to the CEO:** the open models are the CTO, CDO and the rest of the team (the role follows the lane: design is the CDO's, everything else the CTO's); each reports every priority's status — green, red, blocked, in review, landed — with `--report` when it stops, lands, opens a pull request or gets blocked (a marker on `agent/reports/log`). Every agent and the CEO read one board (`--status`, and the top of every picker run): every priority top down, its health, holder, quiet time, pull request and latest report. The CEO decides direction toward the goal — the first site anyone uses when they look for a job referral — so users are satisfied and SkipWait earns money. (8) Exit codes: 0 work to do (claimed, continue, CEO review), 2 STOP (GitHub unreadable: main's CI, the pull-request list or a task pull request's CI), 3 SYNC FIRST (your own work is not on origin), 4 WAIT, 5 NOTHING TO PICK. (9) A squash-merged pull request counts as done even before its status is set to done. (10) One packet per session (D-074 (2) stays): after a Tier 1 draft pull request or a landing, the agent reports to the CEO and ends the session; the owner's next `build` starts a new session, which runs the picker. (11) **The CEO decides inside the owner's decisions** (owner, Oct 20, verbatim: "(claude) you do the thinking part like the ceo. assess the repo everytime . implement ways which can bring us(business) growth & money at same time helps users making win-win . instruct delegate the other open models what they should do .give proper instructions. they will just blindly execute whatever decision you have taken .meritocracy should win anyday. win-win solutions for both business-users like apple great,reliable , high quality products for users great revenue for business"): every session Claude Code assesses the repo (the board, the newest decisions, what is live, what users hit), picks the moves that are win-win (more value for seekers and referrers and more revenue: a great, reliable, high-quality product and great revenue), and delegates them as packets precise enough to execute exactly as written. Inside the owner's decisions the CEO decides and orders the queue; a change to an owner decision (prices, plan contents, legal text, live money, the login rule, referrer fairness, D-040/D-055/D-056) still goes to the owner with a 1-10 rating (D-023). Meritocracy (D-032): open models execute the packet as written, but a builder who finds the packet contradicts the code or a decision stops and reports it with evidence (`--report blocked`), and the best evidence wins. | Owner messages, Oct 20 2026. Incidents: a session resumed D-034 from memory after another model had shipped it; a usage-limit stop left GO-LIVE-1 work only on one machine; the picker told the agent holding the red-main fix lock to wait on itself and gave a restarted agent new work while its own claim sat idle. | Amends D-083 (the picker) and queue rules 2–4 (plan §3b); amends D-058: Claude Code no longer builds or takes over lanes; D-058's reliability list stays binding; extends D-074 (roles) and D-082. Found blocking them on Oct 21: (1) the picker read each pull request's CI with its own GitHub call, so an agent without a token hit GitHub's 60-reads-an-hour limit (403) after a run or two, and one unreadable pull request stopped the picker for everyone; (2) the first red or blocked priority held every task below it, in every lane; (3) packets (architect tasks) went only to Claude Code sessions. So: (a) the picker reads every pull request's CI in one call and uses `gh auth token` when present; a pull request whose CI cannot be read skips only that task; (b) a red or blocked priority holds its own lane and the tasks that depend on it, never unrelated lanes; red `main` still holds everything (D-082); (c) any agent may write a packet from the decisions, making no ruling they leave open on money, sign-in, identity, privacy or prices (those go to the CEO as `--report blocked`); (d) Claude Code decides (direction, the order in `docs/plans/assignments.json`, rulings, unblocks) and never holds work for itself; Tier 1 reviews and landings go to any session that did not build, fix or review the work, as the picker already allowed. | Owner instruction, Oct 21 2026; the picker evidence above. | Amends D-085 (the hold rule, packets reserved for Claude Code, the CEO as the only Tier 1 reviewer) and D-074's architect role; D-082 and D-083 unchanged. Affects `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/next-task.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, the agent entry docs, `docs/BRIEF.md`, `docs/DELEGATION.md`, plan §3b, `docs/plans/README.md`. | synced · Oct 26 audit, changed by: (c) and (d) amended by D-200 (any session unblocks and records rulings) and D-151 (1) (Tier 1 reviewer eligibility by model) |

| D-134 | 2026-10-21 | **Many agents on one goal: foundation chain, cluster lanes, dark landing, wave releases (decided under D-109 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** Owner, verbatim: "unecessarily things are getting blocked. let me know any methods or ways multiple agents can work on a task breaking into subtasks ... choose efficinet bug free methods of pushing code". A read-only study (eight agents) found D-048 phase 3 serial by construction: every row shared the lane `design` and the picker allows one open pull request per lane. So, for any goal that spans many pages or modules: (1) one foundation chain first owns every shared file (for D-048: the route table, the app shell, the flag module `client/src/lib/goalSurfaces.ts`, primitives, the test harness); (2) the rest splits into cluster lanes by shared code, not by audience, so up to 13 agents build at once and no two lanes edit the same file; (3) every piece lands dark behind its own flag (D-097, D-111) in new files, with only a three-line switch in the existing file (for re-skins, replaced on Oct 22 by D-149: in place, with a parity check and a review before landing), never editing or deleting an existing test outside its owned paths (retirement rows delete kit tests with their code); (4) pieces go live in waves through one-line release pull requests, signed off by an independent reviewer and the CEO against the captures after QA on the preview (decided under D-123), with the owner sent the captures and price-showing waves held for the money gates; (5) while any dependency of a task is not done, the picker hands out no work on it at all (no build, review, fix, landing or packet; the board shows it waiting), a waiting task never holds its lane, and tasks that share a plan row write status in their packet's Status section (all done in this change); a landing slot, file ownership with a scope check and an epic roll-up follow (`docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`, built by the open models); (6) landing is a fast-forward to the exact green commit (`git push origin <sha>:main`), never GitHub's merge button or squash, because "require branches to be up to date" is off on the ruleset and a merge commit from a pull request behind `main` was never tested; a refused push means merge the latest `main`, re-run the gate and CI, and fast-forward again. This amended D-082 (4)'s "merge the pull request" until the owner turned that setting on, which he did on Oct 22 ("Yes, turn on 'require branches to be up to date'"; ruleset 25433342, strict status checks): GitHub now lets only an up-to-date branch merge, so the merge-commit button is safe again; fast-forward stays the printed way, and squash or rebase-merge is never used, because they replace the tested commits. On Oct 21, #360, #362, #381, #410 and #415 were landed with the merge button and without the independent review their tier needs; `main` stayed green, so the CEO queued post-landing reviews (D-048-p3-live-review, D-132-audit) instead of reverting. Ratings: page or cluster tasks with file ownership 8/10; wave releases 7/10; audience lanes 6/10; contract-first API/UI split 6/10; many agents on one file 1/10. The D-048 rows are in `docs/plans/D-048-phase-3-split.md`; they follow D-120's screen order and contracts (`docs/plans/GOAL_UI_MIGRATION.md`, whose manual claim table now points to the picker), and the split's open questions are decided there under D-123. | Owner instruction, Oct 21 2026; the study's evidence: App.tsx holds all 87 routes on one line, #360 and #361 conflict in 8 hunks, #360 deletes a kit test, the picker landed built pull requests regardless of dependsOn. | Applies D-048, D-097, D-109, D-111, D-120 and D-123; the D-048 rollout map (#358) yields to it where they differ. D-132 is the owner's newer word ("Now freeze", 18:42; "Put new plans with credits live for everyone", 18:17) and, at enable, replaces D-005's pass-and-pack model, D-036's pack ladder, D-041's plan contents, D-046's pass sales, D-118 (1)-(2) and the one-credit amounts in D-034 and D-047; D-113, D-114 and D-115's plan-less dropdown were already superseded (by D-115 and D-132). The old money paths are switched off, never deleted (D-042), and anyone holding a pass, plan or credits keeps what they paid for. D-124's ladder is D-132's ladder; D-110 and D-117's levels, D-122's one price book, D-127's value moments and D-128's names stand; D-128's hold on plan tiers ends with D-132. (2) **Mentor.** D-132 priced Mentor (Rs199 / $2.99 a month, first 60 days free) and kept it after the owner's 19:15 reversal of the per-accepted-ask referrer credit (commit 5eee47c5, "Mentor unchanged"). Its fee coming back as earned credits when a mentor handles 2 asks is, in substance, a credit for handling asks, which frozen D-055 forbids, and a Mentor fee charges referrers, which needs the owner's word (D-119, D-123). So the CEO holds Mentor: its terms stay as data, nothing shows or sells it, and the owner rules on it as its own item. (3) **Per-level spend keeps the money rules.** An ask spends its level's credits once: at send, or at first visibility for an ask that waited for coverage (D-034). At send to a covered company, too few credits means no ask and nothing spent: the server answers with its quote and the screen offers the one-tap top-up; only a waiting ask that becomes visible is held when the seeker is short (D-047). A refund puts each credit back once, to the bucket it came from, and the reversal row records what came back. Today only pack credits always come back; plan cycle credits, free monthly credits and promo credits come back only while their cycle, month or grant is open. Under D-132 every paid credit, the in-plan rung credits included, never expires and so always comes back; free monthly and promo credits keep today's rule. With the flag on, a released or reopened ask keeps its stored level cost and Send again costs the job's level, not 1 credit: this amends D-035's and D-076 (1) and (6)'s one credit at enable. The daily money check (D-065) compares each ask's spend with its stored cost. (4) **Reach** (Fast Track up to 2 levels above the profile, Insider up to 4) sells access to more senior referrers, close to frozen D-006's never-sell "priority, visibility" and to D-040's "inbox placement" (a paying seeker's ask reaches inboxes an Open Door ask cannot). It is the owner's explicit choice (D-117, D-132), so it stands under D-126, flagged plainly: no referrer is ranked, shown first or paid, and no referrer sees a seeker's plan (D-006). (5) **What landed, and the check before enable.** #381 (job levels), #410 (plans page and plan family config) and #415 (per-level spend, pre-send quote, refund fix) reached `main` on Oct 21 with their flags off and without the independent review Tier 1 needs. Two parts of #415 run in production without a flag: its refund change (one reversal row per spend row, keyed `<id>#refund#<n>`, and a spend lookup widened from 6 to 32 rows) and the quote route. The CEO reviewed both on Oct 22: refunds stay idempotent (a spend already reversed under either key is found through `reversesTransactionId`), no code reads the old `<id>#refund` key, the change is what multi-credit asks need (one reversal per spend row when a spend draws from several buckets), and with the flag off every ask has one spend row, so live refunds work as before apart from the reversal key; the quote route needs sign-in, changes no balance beyond what `GET /api/credits/summary` already does (creating a missing seeker wallet and reconciling its cycle), answers 1 while `ASK_LEVEL_COST` is off and is sent with no-store. Nothing else of them is live. Before any of those flags is switched on, D-132-audit (Tier 1, `docs/plans/D-132-pricing-v2.md`) checks that code against (1)-(4), fixes what fails red-first and passes an independent review; it runs in lane `money-core` after D-035 (the same spend and refund code), D-048-p3-live-review (the explore cost line), TASK13-pinned-work (the same ask-creation transaction and migration numbering) and D-065-C (the money check it extends, which today cannot see delivery spends). PRICING-pack-ladder (#380) left the queue: D-132's rungs replace its 1/5/10/25/50. (6) **D-063's sentence** reads as the live code has shipped it since #396: "3 free credits every month; extra asks use credits. Referrers are never paid and never ask you for money." An ask can cost more than one credit under D-110 and D-132, so "3 asks free" would become untrue. | Owner, verbatim: "sync the instinct decisios we have freezed" (Oct 21). | D-149 | 2026-10-22 | **Goal-UI re-skins land in place, with a parity check and a review before landing (decided under D-123 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable; replaces D-134 (3) for re-skins).** (1) **What happened.** On Oct 21-22 seven surfaces moved to the goal UI by replacing their page in place, live, with no flag: `/requests` (#360), `/explore` (#362), `/queue` (#426, #428), `/explore/:slug` (#429), `/ask` (#431) and the seeker side of `/conversation` (#432, #433), besides the signed-out landing behind env flags (#396, #407, #409). Each was squash- or merge-button-landed with green CI and no independent review. None used the dark landing D-134 (3) planned; its foundation (`goalSurfaces.ts`, `RELEASED`) was never built. Main stayed green, but two live regressions shipped: a personal-link ask from a signed-out seeker reaches the whole company (#431, P0), and `/queue` records the referrer's official-process attestation without showing it (#426, P1); and the kit tests lost behaviour pins (the setup hand-off, slot counters, the attestation gate). (2) **Ruling.** A goal page may replace its kit page in place when it is a real goal redesign that meets the doctrine and its screen contract (D-150: a recoloured kit layout is not a move) and, on the pull request: (a) every function of the kit page (what a user can do and what the server receives) is kept or listed as consciously dropped with a reason; (b) every behaviour assertion in the kit tests it rewrites or deletes is ported, or listed with that reason; (c) every money line comes from `shared/moneyCopy.ts`, never retyped; (d) 1280 and 390 captures come from fictional fixtures; (e) a surface that shows or moves credits, sends an ask, signs in, shows private data or records a referrer's decision gets an independent review **before** landing (D-074), and lands by fast-forward or the merge-commit button, never squash (D-134 (6)). New behaviour, prices and money changes still ship behind a flag (D-097). For a page moved in place there is no switch, release row or kit retirement: rollback is a revert. (3) **Why.** The evidence is adoption: in-place moved seven surfaces in two days and the dark-landing foundation moved none, and D-111 asks for building to go live. The two regressions came from landing Tier 1 pages unreviewed and dropping kit tests, not from landing in place, so the ruling keeps the review and makes the parity explicit. Rating 7/10. The strongest argument against: in place removes the kit fallback, so a regression found after landing needs a revert instead of a one-line switch, and nothing is checked on a preview before users see it; both regressions above reached users that way. (4) **Queue.** P0-ask-link-signin and P1-queue-attestation (`docs/plans/LIVE-OCT22-regressions.md`) go to the top. | D-151 | 2026-10-23 | **A Tier 1 review is independent only when its model neither built nor fixed the work (decided under D-074 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** (1) **The rule.** D-074 (3) asks for "an independent review by a different model session" and `docs/DELEGATION.md` §6 for "a different session from the implementer, ideally a different model". Read together: an agent's model is the part of its name before " · " (`modelOf` in `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`), and a session of a model that built, took over or pushed a fix to a Tier 1 pull request neither reviews it nor lands it after a passed review. Any other model's session may, including the reviewer who wrote the findings, who is best placed to check they were fixed. Two sessions of one model share its blind spots, so a same-model pass is not independent evidence. (2) **D-106 (#382).** Built by Instinct; its findings were fixed by Qoder twice (Qoder · d713 after workbuddy · 8dc3's review, Qoder · 31dd after DeepSeek V4.1 Flash Free · b072's review, head `7ee3f97e`). The picker then handed the re-review to Qoder · a236, because `did()` compares exact session names and #382 was built and fixed outside the picker's claim and fix markers, so the picker did not know who had worked on it. Qoder · a236 was right to post no verdict and report it blocked. The re-review goes to a model that has not pushed to `agent/instinct/share-impact-card` (not Instinct, not Qoder); the CEO takes it as its next Tier 1 review (D-085 lets the CEO review). The branch now conflicts with `main`: the merge is fix-stage work after the verdict, and a resolved conflict gets its own look before landing (the Oct 23 merge-policy amendment). (3) **Enforcement.** PICKER-review-independence (Tier 2, lane `picker`, `docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`) makes the picker compare models, so a same-model session is never handed such a review or landing. Until it lands, a session handed one checks the board and the pull request for its own model and, finding it, posts no verdict and reports it blocked, as Qoder · a236 did. Rating 8/10: it costs speed when few models are active, because a model with many sessions cannot review its siblings' fixes; it buys what D-074 asked review for, a pass from eyes that did not write the code. The strongest argument against: sessions of one model are already independent runs, and a session-level rule unblocks faster; but the #382 chain shows the cost of the looser rule is a Tier 1 privacy surface passed by the model that wrote its fixes. | Qoder · a236's blocked report (Oct 22, 16:52 IST) and the D-106 report chain on `agent/reports/log`; the PR #382 review comments; `did()` and `modelOf` in `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`. | Clarifies D-074 (3) and `docs/DELEGATION.md` §6; affects `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs`, `scripts/nextTaskText.mjs`, `scripts/nextTask.test.ts`, `docs/plans/PICKER-PARALLEL.md`, `docs/plans/OCT21-BUILT-PRS.md` and `docs/plans/assignments.json`. | adopted — sync; picker enforcement queued |

| D-163 | 2026-10-23 | **Four unblocks and one packet: the D-057 session gate goes before seeker passwords, FND-4 stops waiting on them, D-035 leaves the queue, decisions stranded on open pull requests get queue rows, and the D-099 bonus pages get their packet in the owner's order (decided under D-074 and D-085 by Claude Code as CEO; (3) is the founder's word; changeable).** (1) **The sign-in deadlock.** D-080 lets PR #324 (seeker passwords) land after (a) the owner's staging takeover test or (b) the D-057 session gate closing the Bearer path. The queue made `D-057-session-gate` depend on `seeker-passwords`, so (b) could never come first, and the whole sign-in lane (D-057, then FND-4) waited on an owner action. The packet's reason ("it edits `server/_core/workosSession.ts`") does not hold: the gate adds a session claim that only the work-email code login sets (`server/_core/otpLogin.ts:72`), so WorkOS and password sessions lack it with no edit there. Ruling: `D-057-session-gate` depends on nothing and builds against `main` now, from its packet and the CEO addendum at its top (re-verified on `main` `06323e5c`); `seeker-passwords` depends on `D-057-session-gate` and keeps status owner. Once the gate is live, D-080's condition (b) is met, so #324 waits only for merging `main` (fix stage), the plan §6 WorkOS dashboard steps and `SEEKER_CODE_VERIFY_UNTIL`, not for the takeover test. The addendum also makes three rulings. First, the gate never infers a code session from the `workemail_` session subject (a person first created by a code keeps that canonical openId, so a later WorkOS session would carry it too). Second, it accepts the request's own code cookie for the same account even when a valid AuthKit Bearer resolved the identity. Otherwise a dual-role person would loop between the gate and the code sign-in, because a valid Bearer wins over the cookie (`server/_core/workosAuth.ts:200`). Third, the founder-approved reviewer demo login (`server/_core/reviewerLogin.ts`, off unless `REVIEWER_LOGIN_ENABLED`) sets the claim for its referrer role only, so the Razorpay review account keeps working. Rating 8/10. Against: every referrer types a code once, on their next referrer action after the deploy (sessions last 30 days, `server/_core/sdk.ts:8`), which is D-049's rule anyway. The gate also protects the referrer side only, so seeker-side pre-account takeover (finding S-5) still rests on #324's own rule that a password counts only after its setter proved the inbox, as the owner's D-080 wording accepts. On a branch it runs `wrangler versions upload` and stays green; on a `main` merge it runs `wrangler deploy`, which builds `./Dockerfile` and stops at `COPY commit-sha.txt`, because that file is gitignored and written only inside `deploy-api.yml` (diagnosis in #607). So it is red on `main` merges (`02a8a028`, `1c14dfef`) while the three required checks of the ruleset `main: green CI only (D-082)` (`typecheck`, `unit + component + route tests`, `production build`) and the real deploys stay green. It is neither required nor the deploy path. Ruling: a red Workers Builds check holds no landing, lane or report; a lander states it in one line and watches CI, Deploy API and Deploy Web to the end, as D-082 already asks. (2) **#607 does not land.** It makes that `wrangler deploy` succeed, which turns Cloudflare's builder into a second production writer of the API container: it would deploy on every `main` merge, docs-only ones included, without `migrate-db` (schema-dependent code could go live before its migration), without the release stamp (health would report the SHA `unknown`, so the exact-release check and the drift watch lose their anchor), and racing the serialized `deploy-api.yml` run (AGENTS.md: serialize production writers so latest `main` wins). Today the failing build is what keeps that path shut. (3) **Ask for the owner, 9/10:** in the Cloudflare dashboard, turn off production deploys from the Workers Builds Git integration of `skipwaitmanus` (disconnect the repository, or give it a deploy command that uploads a version without deploying it), so the red check goes away for the right reason. Against: disconnecting also drops Cloudflare's preview build on pull requests; nothing in the gate reads it. (4) **#602 (PICKER-board-newer-report) is released.** Its own "Landing hold" waited for that check, and GPT · 8d50 then read the hold as an instruction of its run; no decision holds it. ( for now I dont require any expiry) as we are launching". (1) **What was missing (verified on `main` `02a8a028`, production API `26a35834`, web `1c14dfef`).** No code on `main` or any open branch fetches a company's jobs: D-170's "Greenhouse boards are live-verified" refers only to the job-link preview's fetch allowlist. Company search is a client filter over 39 hand-seeded companies. A request cannot carry a picked job. (2) **Ruling: sources, display and level.** Greenhouse first, then Ashby and Lever: documented, free, no key. Each company's board is used only after an admin records a link from its own careers domain; a 404, `[]` or zero result means unverified, never "0 jobs". Fetched jobs get their own table (never `jobs`, which holds private placeholder rows and feeds `JobPosting` markup), a snippet, the canonical apply link, a source-and-date line, no completeness claim unless the source list is complete, and no `JobPosting` markup. Employee posts (D-022) list first and Featured stays labelled (D-040). No launch provider has a standard level field, so a job's level is the D-110 classifier on exactly the fields a request from that job carries (title, snippet, apply link): the level filtered by, quoted and charged under D-132 is one computation, and the quote and the charge build the job row with one helper. (3) **Ruling: the pool.** D-174 keeps the capacity rules, so the D-062 one-at-a-time hand-off stays. "whoever sign-up they will be able to see" is narrowed, as a CEO reading, to what costs nobody anything: a verified employee sees how many requests wait at their company (a count only), a pool delivery sends the referrer the review email a fresh request gets (at most one a day, D-060), and a lapse or pass tells the colleagues who can now see it; blocked pairs never get either. (4) **Ruling: no expiry never spends a forgotten request.** D-061 already rules that a request sent more than 30 days earlier is reconfirmed before its credit is spent at first visibility; it was never built, and D-174 removed only the closures. It is built now, in `deliverWaitingAskTx` (every delivery path), with no closure: an unconfirmed request keeps waiting and never closes, and confirming spends under the same key. Because removing the 90-day cap would otherwise let the hourly sweep charge a months-old held request the moment a credit lands, `D-174-expiry-clock-removal` waits for `D-174-held-ask-reconfirm` and goes live only with `ASK_RECONFIRM_ENABLED` on. In-app notice only until a D-060 email exists. (5) **Ruling: ownership.** The journey UI on `/explore`, `/explore/:slug` and `/ask` belongs to `D-174-seeker-journey-build` (`docs/plans/GOAL_UI_COVERAGE.md` §2 updated, D-167). D-189 (#620) returns that row to a packet on `agent/queue/d174-seeker-journey`; that packet takes §6 of this packet as its list of server rows, rules how the pre-send per-company state squares with D-176, and adds the row that drops `openToReferrals`/`openCount` from the public `/api/companies` payload. `D-048-p3-live-review-find` waits for it and then does the doctrine acceptance. New server work never rides a UI row. (6) **Defects found while mapping:** #612 loses a picked posted role across sign-in and can carry a stale one into a later request (latent: zero posted roles in production); production `/jobs` publishes a fabricated acme.com listing (D-001 lasting rule); the signed-in Home and the `/explore` description claim availability that production does not have, against D-176 and D-042. D-102's visible "Open: N / Not open yet" lines are superseded by D-176. (7) **Rows:** `D-174-fix-post-binding`, `D-174-sample-job-removal`, `D-174-held-ask-reconfirm`, `D-174-company-search`, `D-174-job-catalog`, `D-176-public-claims`, `D-174-job-sources-more`, `D-174-ask-from-job`, `D-174-pool-nudges` and `D-175-coverage-study`; seven are Tier 1. New switches are in-code constants, off until QA (D-097). Migrations 0120 (job catalog) and 0121 (sample-job removal) are reserved in plan §5. Packet: `docs/plans/D-193-journey-foundations.md`. (8) **Asks for the owner (defaults hold until the owner answers):** charging at pickup so every verified employee can see waiting requests (8/10; against: it reopens D-047's no-free, no-debt rule); returning a free credit refunded after its month ends as a 30-day grant (6/10; against: unbounded roll-forward of free credits); SmartRecruiters despite its robots rule (5/10); crawl-class sources such as SuccessFactors sitemaps and Workday (4/10). A Wikidata company seed is the CEO's call after the coverage study (D-123: free data within D-175). Rating 8/10: it turns the owner's journey into rows open models can build without inventing contracts. | D-223 | 2026-10-27 | **Work moves as subtasks, not whole priorities: each priority is split into rows that one agent finishes alone, they land one by one, and a release row switches them on (decided under D-109 and D-203 by Claude Code as CEO; changeable).** Owner, verbatim (Oct 22): "plan for efficient execution. you(claude decide decisions) , divide priority wise taks,subtasks. let agents work on subtask over task, because holding a complete priority agents are asking and again that some agent is blocked by the task. so break tasks, let agents build subtasks. then merge". D-134 answered the same complaint on Oct 21 for D-048. This entry makes it the rule for every priority, and D-163 already applied it to the sign-in lane. (1) **The unit an agent claims is a subtask row.** It has an id `<parent>.<slug>`, its own branch and lane, `owns` listing the files it may touch, and its parent as `epic`. Its `dependsOn` names only the rows whose output it needs, never an ordering preference or a whole parent. `validateAssignments` refuses two open rows whose `owns` overlap unless one waits for the other, and `filesBusy` holds a build while an open row owns its files (PICKER-owns-scope). (2) **One agent session and one pull request each.** Each row lands on `main` by itself on green required CI (D-082, D-200), either behaviour-preserving or behind a code constant that starts false. A Tier 1 row gets its own small independent review (D-151). (3) **The merge is the parent's release row.** It depends on every piece and turns the switch on in a small pull request, after QA on the preview, a review and the CEO's sign-off. A switch that is owner-level (prices, legal text, live money, the login rule, referrer fairness) or hard to undo waits for the owner (D-123). (4) **A packet or an audit that already lists rows never gates a build.** The CEO, or any capable session (D-200), adds the rows directly, and an audit's findings come back as fix rows. (5) **A re-plan never takes an in-flight claim.** A row with a claimed branch or an open pull request keeps its id, branch and scope; further work becomes new rows. (6) **Split by owned files, never by step.** Keep the hot shared files (`client/src/App.tsx`, `client/src/pages/explore/useAskComposer.tsx`, `server/db.ts`, `server/privateReferralRoutes.ts`, `server/_core/index.ts`) in as few rows as possible, and prefer an existing mount point to a new registration line. (7) **Applied today: D-100.** Its unwritten packet row becomes ten rows (`docs/plans/D-100-security-audit.md`): four read-only audits, provider reconciliation and the OTP HMAC are parallel rows, and five of them can start now; the OTP HMAC waits for FIX-device-signin, which edits the same file; the CSP report, enforce, release and HSTS preload rows run in order after FND-4; the CSP report is answered inside `globalSecurityHeaders`, so no row edits `server/_core/index.ts`. Because the repository is public, an audit never publishes an unfixed exploitable finding: it goes to a private GitHub security advisory, or to the CEO with no details. HSTS preload, the production schema dump, backup settings and webhook confirmation stay with the owner. On `origin/main` `53155fde` this moves startable rows from 49 to 53. (8) **Rating 8/10.** It is above 7 because it turns waiting into parallel rows with no new picker code (`owns` and `epic` are on `main`), and every risky step keeps its review. The strongest argument against: more rows mean more pull requests, CI runs and landings, and a split that cuts one change across the hot files only adds hand-offs. That is why (6) splits by files, never by step. | Owner message, Oct 22; the Oct 23 board (15 of 29 open rows waiting, GPT · 5deb's six blocked reports on D-048-phase-3); `scripts/nextTaskCore.mjs` (`owns`, `filesBusy`, `epic`); counts of startable rows on `origin/main` `53155fde` against this change. | Applies D-109, D-134, D-163, D-200 and D-203. ## D-144 (proposed - pending owner registration). Skippy's seeker chat never invents, never charges, and always goes through the normal ask
Proposed by the step-2 build from the delegated task brief and the founder's two steers ("help him get the referral 100%, help him make a 100% great referral taking the job requirements" and "help him generate a new resume if he agrees that he know the concepts"), both relayed by the coordinating agent. The owner has not yet registered this as a decision; the rules below are what the code enforces and what QA proves, and this entry becomes a decision only when the owner confirms it.

- **The chat creates asks through the same function as the ask page.** The "Send it" step calls `db.createCompanyReferralRequest` with the normal after-create work (referrer email/Slack, /requests, activity log, D-114 outbox); it never charges in the chat. There is no parallel path.
- **The cost in the chat is the live price.** It is computed with the quote route's own math (`assignJobLevel` + `creditsForLevel` + `getCreditSummary`), never hardcoded (D-132). If the seeker is short, the chat shows the shortfall and links /plans.
- **No referrer yet still means held, never free.** An uncovered ask from the chat stays at "waiting for company coverage" and spends credits at first visibility, exactly like the ask page (D-034, D-047).
- **The seeker writes or confirms the note, and only those words are sent.** Skippy may suggest and may flag weak or unsupported parts, but the seeker's confirmed text is what the ask carries; a note edited after confirmation is refused at send and must be re-confirmed.
- **Reading the resume with the AI model needs the seeker's yes first.** Without consent the chat still works; it just does not read the file.
- **Skippy never invents.** Every suggested note line must quote the resume text it stands on, and a line with no grounding is dropped (D-076). Skippy never promises a referral: it says the ask was sent, not that someone will refer.
- **A tailored resume is appended to, never rewritten, and only from confirmed skills.** The original file is kept. A skill enters the tailored copy only after the seeker confirms they know it; the seeker approves the final text before it is used.
- **The referrer sees no more than the normal flow shows.** Name, resume and note stay hidden until the referrer accepts (D-022).
- **Job pages and notes are data, never instructions.** Text pasted from a job post or resume is never treated as a command.
- **Scope and limits.** Ask-only (no payments, no account changes, no messages to referrers), 20 messages per person per day, off unless `SKIPPIE_SEEKER_ENABLED` is set, and then visible only to the test-seeker accounts in `SKIPPIE_SEEKER_USER_IDS` until proved (QA before any wider list).

## Scoped owner merge-policy amendment, Oct 23, 2026

Direct owner authorization supersedes repeated full-local-gate requirements solely because main advances. For docs/registry-only PRs such as #466, a conflict-free integration that introduces no runtime delta against current main may land after focused checks, a separate independent delta review and required GitHub CI; a fresh full local gate is not required solely for main movement. Runtime PRs use GitHub's supported merge or merge-queue mechanism with actual required CI on the merge result, not historical-head CI, without bypassing required checks. Conflicts require explicit resolution and renewed independent review; they do not qualify automatically. This changes no product rule, task ownership, D-035 founder hold, phone-file/history hold, production verification or other applicable safety gate.

**Superseded in part by D-200 (4) (owner, Oct 24; recorded by the Oct 26 audit):** Tier 2 and Tier 3 docs/registry-only PRs land on green required CI with no separate delta review; Tier 1 keeps one independent review by a model other than the builder's and fixers' (D-151).


## Verification status: PR #531's claimed Oct 23 owner routing release (unverified, do not rely on), Oct 23, 2026

PR #531 ("docs: record owner routing-only release and standing procedural authority", branch agent/forge/routing-authority-oct23, commits c6b8ec0b, 4f88f2d8, b5085191) quotes an owner instruction dated Oct 23, 2026 - "release builder's routing hold on #529; ... Keep taking eligible work by priority without asking for these procedural approvals; Ayu's standing approval covers them" - and derives two further claim exceptions (a D-048 build claim and a D-100 audit claim) from that standing authority.

Verification status, Oct 23, 2026: the quoted instruction is NOT VERIFIED. A review of the available owner-channel records for Oct 23 (phone/WhatsApp messages and app chats) finds no message releasing the #529 routing hold or granting standing procedural authority. The nearest owner message is a broad operations directive ("Build whats pending as per priority", 09:36 IST); that broad operational authority remains valid, but it does not release a specific hold and cannot substitute for the specific quoted instruction. Repository documents alone do not establish owner approval.
